Introduction
Welcome to Elegy                                         What You Need to Play
Elegy is a solitaire roleplaing game (a solo RPG)        Dice
where you play as a young vampire surviving in
a big city. You seek renown among your peers,            Elegy uses only d6s and d10s. 1d6 and 2d10 is the
fight to remain human, and uncover the secrets           minimum. However, some specific situations may
of a dark and unfamiliar world. Through the              require more dice, so having 1 or 2 extra of each is
Connections you form with other characters and           ideal. Additionally, there are a few percentile rolls;
the missions you take on (whether for them or            if you’d like, you can also get a d100.
for yourself) you will give new meaning to your
existence.                                               Sheets
You will create a character and a world for her          These are the main sheets you’ll use to track im-
to inhabit, and follow her story as you control          portant game information:
her actions in the narrative you envision. When
exploring, investigating, and interacting with the       • Character Sheet (two-sided): For your
world, other characters, or elements beyond your           character’s core traits and characteristics.
control, you’ll use dice to randomly shape and           • World Sheet (two-sided): To record the
expand what happens around her.                            basics of your city, important places, political
                                                           groups and power figures, as well as your
As your story unfolds, your vampire will evolve,           worldbuilding choices.
and her skills and abilities will grow more potent       • Connection Sheet: To track your important
and diverse. You can also record the narrative in          relationships.
any way that interests you, whether as a personal        • Mission Sheet: To track your objectives and
journal, short stories, or simply a list of bullet         your progress in reaching them.
points on sticky notes.                                  • Loose Ends Sheet: To record the unsolved
                                                           questions you're left with in your story.
Multiplayer                                              • Adversary Sheet: To track adversaries'
Elegy is primarily a solo game, but you can play           durability in fights.
with others if you wish. There are two multiplayer       You need one Character Sheet per player, and
modes:                                                   one World Sheet for the whole game.Every player
• Guided: One or more players take on the                starts with at least one of the others, and may need
  roles of protagonists, while a gamemaster              more as the game progresses. All these sheets can
  (GM) moderates the session. The GM brings              be found in the end of this manual.
  the world to life, portrays the characters
  you meet, and decides the outcome of your
  actions.
• Cooperative (Co-Op): You and one or more
  friends play together to overcome challenges
  and complete missions. No GM required.

Something to Write With                                     The Setting
While not absolutely required, it’s highly recom-
mended that you keep notes about your narrative             Elegy takes place in a world much like our own,
so you don’t forget anything important.                     except vampires exist. They survive by feeding on
You don’t need to write more than you want to.              human blood, keep their existence secret by blend-
                                                            ing among us, and live in a treacherous society
The key is to make sure it helps your immersion and         where power, status, and influence are paramount.
keeps track of what matters to you. You can use what-
ever format works best: a short story, a screenplay,        Elegy is a game about being a scary creature in a
a first-person journal, or even concise bullet lists.       scary city, but also about humanity and the drive
                                                            to survive.
What's this Manual?
                                                            Inspiration
This manual is the beta version of the fourth edi-
tion rules for Elegy.                                       Elegy’s setting draws from:

It introduces the new mechanics while the official          • Tabletop RPGs: Vampire: The Masquerade,
material is still in development, allowing players            Vampire: The Requiem
to play, test, and provide feedback.                        • Fiction: Carmilla, Dracula
                                                            • Video games: VtM: Bloodlines
The rules here will mostly remain as presented,             • Film and TV: Blade, Only Lovers Left Alive,
but minor updates and refinements may occur                   What We Do in the Shadows
based on playtesting and feedback.                          • Classic vampire myths and folklore
The 4th Edition (4e) rulebook will include flavor
text, original artwork, story hooks, gameplay tips,
and other extras.




E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )                6
Your Character
In Elegy, you play a vampire whose background you create. Your character can be of any culture, race,
sexual orientation, gender, personality, or appearance. However, as a vampire, there are things you share
with every other creature of your kind.




        You’re a                                          You’re young and
        bloodthirsty beast.                               inexperienced.
        Your hunger is the most honest thing about        No matter how long you lived as a mor-
        you, and blood is your one true love. Peo-        tal, your unlife started only a year ago.
        ple, relationships, status, and possessions       You may be stronger than almost any hu-
        matter, but none of them gnaw at you from         man, but you’re still weaker than most
        the inside the way blood does. When your          vampires in both power and influence. You
        hunger speaks, everything else becomes            need relationships and alliances to survive.
        noise.                                            Step too far out of line, and vampire au-
                                                          thorities have the means to destroy you.
        You’re still human.
                                                          You’re a cool
        You have values and desires. You crave
        meaning, connection, pleasure, and your           creature of the night.
        experiences still change you. But your            Being a vampire feels good. Hardly any
        humanity constantly clashes with your             living human can harm or overpower you;
        instincts, and your basic needs have a way        you are an apex predator. Your mortal fears
        of putting your principles in perspective.        are gone, and you’ve never felt so free, driv-
                                                          en, and secure. You can rely on yourself
        You’re a wolf in                                  when things get dangerous, difficult, or
        sheep’s clothing.                                 scary.

        You are a predator hiding among prey
        that take you for one of their own, and
        it must stay that way. If mortals discov-
        er what you are, you endanger not only
        yourself, but every vampire around you.

Introduction                                               Your Character’s Basics
Elegy works on two levels: mechanics and narra-            Identity
tive. The mechanics are the gamey part, involving
dice and resources like Rush or Health. The nar-           On the top of your character sheet, you’ll write
rative is the story part: what your character does,        basic information about them.
what happens around them, and how the plot                 • Name: The name you answer to in death. It
unfolds. The two work together. Your narrative               may or may not be the name you had in life.
flows freely until it hits a moment that needs a           • Apparent age: You look just like you did in
mechanical resolution. Once resolved, the dice’s             death. This is the age you’ll look like forever.
outcome shapes what happens next.                          • Real age: How long have you existed for?
                                                           • Occupation in life: What was your job in life?
Asking the Oracle                                          • Look and vibes: How do you present yourself?
When you have questions about what happens                   Do you have any distinctive physical features?
next, what you discover, or how characters respond,          What’s the first impression you usually give?
you may always go with what feels right and pick           • Progenitor: The vampire who turned you.
the most obvious or interesting result.                    • Possessions: The important things you own.
                                                           • Home: Where do you live and sleep by day?
When you want to surprise yourself or need inspi-
ration, ask the Oracle: use random tables (called          Attributes
oracles; see Oracles) to decide for you. Some rules
will prompt you to ask the Oracle directly.                The basic characteristics of your character are your
The Yes/No Question Oracle is the simplest and             Attributes: Body, Mind, Charm and Soul. The
most versatile. To use it, decide the odds of a yes,       higher the score of an attribute, the more likely
then roll on the table below.                              you are to succeed in related actions.

You can also use it to pick between two options.           • Body measures your physical strength and
Rate one as likely and roll. If the answer is yes,           resistance, agility, and coordination. You
that’s the one. If no, it’s the other.                       mainly use it to fight enemies, overcome
                                                             physical challenges, and resist physical harm.
       Odds            The answer is yes if you roll       • Mind measures your intelligence, sharpness,
                                                             and resolve. You mainly use it to gather or
   Small Chance                 10 or less
                                                             apply knowledge, investigate places or people,
     Unlikely                   25 or less                   and resist despair and horror.
                                                           • Charm represents your persuasiveness, charisma,
       50/50                    50 or less

      Likely                    75 or less                   others and hide your true nature from mortals.

  Almost Certain                90 or less

Aspects                                                     EVOLVING

The particular resources of your character are              As you deepen Connections, complete Missions, and
called Aspects.                                             learn about the world, you gain experience points (XP).
                                                            XP can be spent to upgrade your Aspects or acquire
• Expertises are specific skills your character is          new ones. See the Experience section for details.
  trained and specialized in, like driving, lying,
  or brawling.                                              Two types of Aspects follow their own rules and
• Gifts are groups of vampiric powers, including            are never acquired with XP: Connections develop
  mind control, shapeshifting, or commanding                through their own progress track, and Burdens are
  animals. You can naturally develop up to                  marked when the fiction demands it.
  three, but may acquire more by learning from              NARRATIVE REQUIREMENTS
  other vampires.
• Mysteries are groups of magic powers and,                 When you add an Aspect to your character, consid-
  sometimes, rituals. Mostly they work like                 er the narrative justification. What have you done
  Gifts; however, they are never innate and must            to gain this ability? How have your goals shifted
  always be learned. Thematically, they are also            to support this new focus? Your choice should be
  more “magical” than “vampiric”.                           meaningful in the context of your story.
• Edges are resources or assets of any other                The narrative may also cut against your Aspects. Some-
  nature, like richness, faction loyalty or fame.           times you’ll be in a situation where you can’t leverage
• Burdens are the lasting marks your character              a key ability, and that’s part of what makes the story
  carries when challenges overwhelm them.                   dramatic. Before you act, make sure you’re positioned
• Connections represent your most meaningful                and equipped to use the Aspect you’re relying on.
  or useful relationships.

  them                                                      and can drive the narrative, but they grant no me-

  within its actions

  supernatural power                                        For a superhuman predator, money is easy to ac-

E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )               10
Conditions
Conditions are enduring consequences your character faces when her limits are tested. How long a con-
dition lasts varies. Some fade in moments. Others linger for many nights. Some are permanent: those
are called Burdens, a type of Aspect.

HEALTH                                                   CONSCIENCE

• Wounded: Your corpse struggles to keep                 • Detached: You are momentarily separated
  together, and you must regenerate.                       from your humanity.
• Mangled: You lose a body part; it will regrow,         • Penitent: You are struck by guilt and commit
  but slowly.                                              to a Mission for redemption.
• Scarred: You suffer a wound so severe it leaves        • Blighted: Vampirism further expands its
  a permanent, deep scar.                                  influence on you.

CLARITY                                                  STANDING

• In Shock: You become deeply distressed and             • Cautioned: You are warned by vampire
  must rest.                                               authorities; your reputation is at risk.
• Tormented: You commit to a Mission to avoid            • Discredited: You are shunned or condemned;
  a dreadful event.                                        your standing in the underworld collapses.
• Traumatized: Your psyche has been damaged              • Branded: You anger a vampire elder or
  by the terrors you endured.                              authority so deeply, they permanently brand
                                                           you with hot iron.
BLOOD

• Starving: You can’t ignore your hunger
  anymore, and must feed.
• Enraged: You are completely taken over by
  your violent survival instincts.
• Torpid: Your animating power is permanently
  weakened.


Other characters
Although the mechanics of Elegy revolve almost entirely around your character, you’ll constantly interact
with other characters:
• In multiplayer games, the other players’                 unsure about what an NPC wants or what
  characters are called your allies.                       they’ll do next, ask the Oracle.
• Every character not controlled by a player is          • When you form a meaningful relationship with
  called an NPC (non-player character). Most               an NPC, you gain a Connection with them.
  NPCs have a single stat: their Rank, which             • Political groups of your world are known as
  represents their power and influence. NPCs               Factions. They have no mechanical stats, and
  don’t roll dice to resolve actions; if you’re            in gameplay are represented through fiction.

The Action Roll
When you need to resolve an uncertain or risky                   Results
situation related to your character’s efforts, you
make an action roll. Action rolls all follow the same            The result of your roll depends on how many chal-
structure, described below. Most rolls you make                  lenge die your action score beats.
in Elegy are action rolls. A few very specific rolls             • If your action score beats both challenge dice,
may use different structures, but they are always                  it’s a Stylish Success. You get what you want,
explained where they appear.                                       the way you want.
To make an action roll, roll your action die (1d6)               • If it beats only one, it’s a Flat Success. You get
and two challenge dice (2d10).                                     what you want, but with side effects, reduced
                                                                   effectiveness, or a moderate cost.
                                                                 • If it beats neither, it’s a Failure. Your
                                                                   assumptions were wrong, a brief triumph
                                                                   costs too much, or you simply fail.

  action. When rules tell you to roll with an

E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )                    12
Rush                                                           Paying the Price
Rush represents your inertia and confidence, and               When a rule tells you to pay the price, choose a
how sure you are of your actions. The value ranges             fitting consequence from the table below, or roll
from -6 to +10.                                                on it to decide at random. If the result doesn’t
You use Rush to improve the result of an action                make sense, roll again. The table references re-
roll: after making one, if your Rush is higher than            sources and terms explained later in this chapter
your action score, you may cool your Rush to replace           (see Suffering).
your action score with your current Rush value.
When you do, reset your Rush to the value of your
base Rush (+2 by default). This value, as well as your
max Rush, may change if your character uses certain
abilities, or is transformed through her experiences.

  d100                                                         Price

 01–03     A trusted individual or group acts against you: lose Rush

 04–06     You are separated from something or someone: lose Rush

 07–09     An individual or group you care about is exposed to danger: lose Rush

  10–12    A new adversary is revealed: lose Rush

  13–15    You create an opportunity for an adversary: lose Rush

  16–18    A Connection or companion is harmed: lose Pulse

  19–21    Something of value is lost/destroyed: lose Rush

  22–28    You encounter signs of a looming threat: lose Clarity

 29–35     You face consequences of an earlier choice: roll again or pick another option

 36–42     A Connection or companion is put in danger’s way: lose Rush

 43–49     You spend too much/waste money: lose Rush

 50–56     Your action has an unintended effect: lose Rush

  57–63    You are delayed/disadvantaged: lose Rush

  64–71    You’re harmed: lose Health

  72–79    You’re frightened or despaired: lose Clarity

 80–87     Your action violates vampire laws or customs: Try Your Standing next time you slumber

 88–95     The power you’re activating or is active consumes your life force: lose Blood

 96–00     Roll twice

Best Practices for Action Rolls
FICTION FIRST                                                    ROLLS IN A GROUP

What are you trying to do? How are you doing                     When adventuring with other player charac-

Action rolls should always change the current                    action roll rules unless stated otherwise.
situation.                                                       If only some results are specified (like a Flat Suc-

consequences of her actions, you may…                    sources and Tenets.
• …lose Rush, if facing indirect setbacks;               • Resources (Health, Clarity, Blood, Rush) are
• …lose Health, if enduring physical harm;                 meters that deplete as your character endures
• …lose Clarity, if enduring despair, horror,              punishment. When you “lose” a resource,
  and mental imbalance;                                    lower its meter in accordance with the nature
• …lose Blood, if using the supernatural                   of the situation. When a meter is at 0 and you
  abilities of her blood;                                  would lose more, you risk suffering enduring
• …Try Your Standing, if violating the laws and            consequences called Conditions, or reaching
  customs of the underworld;                               the end of your story. Permanent conditions
• …Try Your Conscience, if performing                      are called Burdens, and are a type of Aspect
  extreme things like taking a human life.                 you can later upgrade.
                                                         • Tenets (Conscience, Standing) have no meter.
                                                           Instead, you roll to hold on to them only when
                                                           specific events challenge them: committing
                                                           an atrocity tries your Conscience; violating
                                                           the customs of the underworld tries your
                                                           Standing. On a Failure, you suffer Conditions
                                                           the same way.

Blood
Blood represents your animating force and satiation gained from consuming blood. The lower it is,
the hungrier you become, and the closer you are to being taken over by instincts or even stop moving.
When you wake up or use a supernatural ability, you lose Blood according to what you’re doing.

Progress Tracks
Longer challenges and goals are tracked using
progress tracks. You use them in three situations:
                                                                       PROGRESS PER RANK

                                                                        Rank 1                      Rank 2

• Mission: tracks the challenges you overcome
  on your path to a goal.
• Connection: tracks the growing trust in a                          Rank 3       Rank 4            Rank 5
  relationship.
• Combat: tracks your advantage over an
  enemy in battle.
When you create a progress track, assign a Rank
reflecting its difficulty. Tracks have ten boxes. As
you make headway, mark progress according to the
Rank; higher Ranks take more effort to complete.

Connections
Creating a Connection
When you start a new relationship with a useful or important character, or when you narratively
establish one you already had, you create a Connection. On your Connection sheet, write their name
and give them an appropriate Rank (see below).
Connections are Aspects, and can be upgraded with XP.

COMMON NPC RANKS
    Type          Rank 1           Rank 2            Rank 3            Rank 4                  Rank 5

  Vampire       Newborn            Young            Mature              Elder                 Ancient

    Fey            —              Refugee           Official             —                      High

or arrangement you like. Once a unit reaches 5             tally structure

Introduction

(defining the “natural” laws of your universe, the            cating and pleasurable for the victim, who drifts
characteristics of your city, and its political land-         into a dazed state for several minutes, unaware
scape) as well as your character. You can keep initial        they were ever bitten.
ideas brief and expand them during play.
                                                              HUNGER
In a multiplayer story, go through the setup togeth-
er. You might spend just a few minutes outlining              Unlike humans, who eat and drink, vampires have
the essentials, or dive deeper with an extended               only one need: to drink blood. This hunger goes
session devoted to worldbuilding. Leave some                  far beyond mortal thirst or starvation. It feels like
questions open and embrace discovery as the game              the absence of safety and survival itself.
unfolds. If you’re the Game Master (GM), guide                More than merely sustaining a vampire, blood
the conversation, contribute to world creation,               ties her to her humanity and to the mortal world.
and take cues from the players to shape quests                Starvation strips that tie away, making vampires
and adversaries.                                              grow violent and instinct-driven, and forcing them
                                                              to face the most honest wishes of the monster
The Basic Setting                                             they are.

Assume the following to be true when building                 By contrast, feeding feels like shelter after a storm,
your world.                                                   bringing strength to endure another night and
                                                              relief from the fear of perishing. For vampires, the
VAMPIRES                                                      act is overwhelmingly pleasurable.
A vampire is a corpse returned to life, sustained             ELEGY
by drinking blood. To hunt, it uses its human
appearance and supernatural abilities. It retains             To fill the void left by death, a loss of everything
its memories, looks, and personality from life, but           they once were and loved, vampires devote them-
becomes dominated by an unrelenting hunger.                   selves to their elegy: a perpetual building of a new
Vampires have a wide array of powers that let them            meaning in their existence.
easily overcome whatever stands between them and              VULNERABILITIES
their prey: hypnosis, invisibility, communion with
animals, fear-inducing presence, or even flight.              Vampires do not die of old age and are only de-
                                                              stroyed by extreme damage. They’re supernatu-
BLOOD                                                         rally resistant to most physical harm and recover
Blood is a vampire’s only source of nourishment.              quickly from injury, though some wounds may
While they prefer the blood of living humans, they            leave permanent effects. However, they are ex-
can survive on less vital options, such as animal             tremely vulnerable to fire and sunlight. At dawn,
blood or stored supplies.                                     even in darkness, they grow lethargic and must
                                                              slumber in a place protected from both mortals
Vampires draw blood through puncture wounds                   and daylight.
made with retractable fangs, which they can later

BLIGHTS                                                      TURNING

Some vampires subvert their kind’s usual nature:             The process of creating a new vampire is called
some lack supernatural powers, some have inhu-               turning. To turn a mortal, a vampire must drain
man looks, some cast no reflection, etc. These               her to death (or kill her by other means), then
traits are called blights. Blights are developed by          feed the fresh corpse a drop of their own blood.
vampires who commit inhuman deeds.                           The newly arisen vampire is called the progeny,
                                                             and the one who made her is called the progenitor.
SOCIETY
                                                             Turning can happen for many reasons. Sometimes,
A vampire’s very existence is a threat to others of          it’s an act of mercy: the only way to save a dying per-
their kind. They may compete for prey, clash over            son. Sometimes, it’s about legacy: the mortal might
goals, or risk exposing the species to mortals. In           one day become a valuable asset. And sometimes, it’s
theory, every vampire would be safest as the only            punishment: a cruel condemnation to eternal unlife.
one in existence. Because of this, vampires tend to
be wary of one another, at least at first.                   The relationship between progenitor and progeny
                                                             varies: it may be loving, jealous, abusive, hostile,
Still, most avoid destroying each other outright,            or entirely absent. Typically, when a vampire is
since violence draws attention. Organized, peace-            turned, they spend one year or so under their
ful coexistence also allows them to grow stronger            progenitor’s tutelage and learn the fundamentals
against human threats. Many vampires join groups             of their new nature, powers, society and culture.
in pursuit of power, enlightenment, or knowledge.            After that, they’re released to survive on their
Despite their supernatural nature, vampires are              own terms, and are finally accepted as vampires.
fewer in number and far less organized than mor-
                                                             VAMPIRE BLOOD
tals. To avoid discovery and extermination, they
strictly avoid revealing their true nature. Violating        Vampire blood is addictive and creates an emotion-
this secrecy carries consequences, from branding to          al bond in whoever drinks it, even other vampires.
execution, depending on the severity of the offense.         Those who consume it feel a deep attachment to
Relationships between vampires and humans, per-              its source. Vampires often share their blood to
sonal or professional, are risky and not always wel-         create loyalty, emotional dependency, or servitude.
comed, but they are allowed, as long as the vampire
never lets the human uncover their true nature.
Rarely, some vampires take no part in the undead
society and maintain no connection with others of
their kind. Cities are extremely large places and
it’s not hard to isolate oneself.

Going Deeper
The options above shape the flavor and assump-
tions of your world. For those who want to go fur-
ther, the game also offers a set of alternative rules
that change how some mechanics work, altering
the basic setting. If you want that extra layer of
customization, see Chapter 7: Alternative Rules.

Step 3: Powers and skills
INNATE GIFTS

Your character has three innate Gifts that may have helped her survive and thrive. Pick them from the
table below, roll to decide randomly, or do a bit of both.

  d100            Gift                                            Description

 01–08         Animality       Powers of commanding animals around you and within others

 09–16          Control        Powers of dominating minds and commanding obedience

 17–24           Decay         Powers of putrefaction and death-like decay

 25–32         Endurance       Powers of superhuman physical resistance

 33–40          Haunt          Powers that instill fear, terror, and despair

 41–48         Lightness       Powers of superhuman balance, agility, gliding, and flight

 49–56         Magnetism       Powers of emotional manipulation and supernatural allure

 57–64           Might         Powers of superhuman physical strength

 65–72         Mutation        Powers that transform you into beasts and elements

 73–79         Shadows         Powers of commanding and shaping supernatural darkness

 80–86           Speed         Powers of superhuman velocity and reflexes

 87–93           Veil          Powers of vanishing and disguising your appearance

 94–00          Vision         Powers of superhuman intuition and perception




                                                    47                          4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )   E LE GY
SKILLS AND ASSETS

She also has skills and assets that helped her thrive in both life and undeath. Pick three Aspects or
roll to decide at random:
1. one Expertise that was her main talent in life, likely something fundamental to her career;
2. one Expertise or Edge that served her well in life and remains very useful in undeath;
3. and one Expertise or Edge representing what she developed or acquired to survive in undeath,
   likely something that compensates for her shortcomings.

   d100             Name                   Type                                 Description

  01–05          Academics            Expertise   Skills in academia, research, and scholarly knowledge

  06–10              Arts             Expertise   Skills in artistic creation

  11–15           Athletics           Expertise   Skills in running, jumping, climbing, and physical feats

  16–20         Computers             Expertise   Skills in using, programming, and hacking computer systems

  21–25          Deception            Expertise   Skills in lying and concealing motives

  26–30            Driving            Expertise   Skills in operating and maneuvering vehicles

  31–35           Empathy             Expertise   Skills in reading emotions and understanding others’ feelings

  36–40           Gunnery             Expertise   Skills in shooting, maintaining, and identifying ranged weapons

  41–45        Investigation          Expertise   Skills in finding clues, solving puzzles, and analyzing evidence

  46–50           Larceny             Expertise   Skills in lockpicking, pickpocketing, and bypassing security

  51–55             Melee             Expertise   Skills in close-quarters combat

  56–60            Nature             Expertise   Skills in navigating the wilderness and understanding animals

  61–65          Persuasion           Expertise   Skills in convincing others through logic and charm

  66–70            Politics           Expertise   Skills in navigating and operating within political structures

  71–75         Sex Appeal            Expertise   Skills in seduction and sexual performance

  76–80        Socialization          Expertise   Skills in fitting in, networking, and navigating social situations

  81–85          Streetwise           Expertise   Skills in navigating the criminal underworld and urban survival

  86–90      Faction Loyalty               Edge   You're an active member of your faction (only available in undeath)

  91–95          Following                 Edge   You are known and admired within a specific subculture

 96–00             Wealth                  Edge   You have a lot of disposable money

Step 7: Finishing Touches
• Set Max Rush to 10, and Base Rush to 2.
• Set your current Rush to 2.
• Set Health and Clarity to 5.
• Set Blood to 4. Vampires spend 1 Blood to sleep through the day, and never awaken fully satisfied.
• Note down the starting ability of each Aspect you picked (see following section).

    Notation suggestion: FyB (Mind): gaze through
    known mirror, GI or SaA once. Extra challenge
    die, drop lowest, if not sure if there’s a mirror there.




E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )                       58
Create your City
In Elegy, your whole story takes place in a single city. It can be a real city, inspired by one, or entirely
fictional. The details about your city are recorded on the “City” side of your World Sheet.

Basics
Picture what this city is like, then fill out the following details on your World Sheet:
• Name: what is your city called officially?
• Founded in: write a date, year, century, or even historical period.
• Economy: what are the main industries and economic activities?
• Climate: subtropical? Temperate? Polar? Something else?
• Language(s): what are the official languages?
• Highlights: what lures mortals into this city?
• Ugly side: what makes them leave?
For the highlight and ugly side, you may roll on the following tables or use them for inspiration. Every
result also presents a detail seed you answer to flesh your city out.

WHAT MAKES A GOOD FIRST MISSION?                           Step 2: Set the Scene
• Make it personal. Why does this matter to                Once your mission is chosen, decide how to begin.
  your character?
• Make it unavoidable. This problem won’t                  OPTION 1: PROLOGUE
  solve itself; it demands a response.                     Start slow. Show your character during a typical
• Add urgency. The situation will get worse or             night: hunting, meeting acquaintances, enjoying
  slip away if ignored.                                    the nightlife, or handling routine tasks. Use this
• Raise the stakes. If it feels too small, layer           to build character and relationships before the
  in complexity, consequences, or emotional                real action begins. After a few scenes, introduce an
  tension.                                                 inciting incident or a request from a Connection
• Keep the scope tight. Focus on something                 that kicks off the mission.
  manageable that could unfold in one or two
  sessions.                                                OPTION 2: IN MEDIAS RES

MULTIPLAYER                                                Start in the middle of the action. The problem
                                                           has already begun, and your character is already
If you’re playing with allies (other player charac-        reacting. You’re diving straight into the thick of it.
ters), this becomes a shared mission. Collaborate          Things are already in motion.
to create the incident and ensure it connects with
your group’s goals and dynamics.                           Step 3: Commit to a Mission
If you’re the game master (GM), introduce a com-
pelling incident based on character and world              Write down the mission and assign it a Rank:
details, or guide the group in co-creating one so          • Rank 1: a simple or minor task.
that everyone is invested.                                 • Rank 2: a challenging but unremarkable task.
                                                           First missions should be Rank 1 or 2; higher Ranks
                                                           are for later in the story.

                                                           Step 4: Next Steps
                                                           After committing to your mission, consider the
                                                           context. Is the path clear? Did new questions arise?
                                                           Is something now standing in your way?
                                                           From here, play to discover. Ask questions. React
                                                           to developments. Roll dice when needed. When in
                                                           doubt, ask the Oracle. Don’t worry about getting
                                                           everything right. Mistakes and uncertainty are part
                                                           of learning. Keep your reference sheets handy, im-
                                                           provise when needed, and keep the game moving.

C H A P T E R   5



Other
Characters
NPCs
NPCs (non-player characters) are everyone in the              Creating NPCs
world who isn’t you or your fellow players (your
allies). You roleplay interactions and attempt to             Build NPCs that fit your city and world. You can
influence, aid, gain help, or act against them.               start with sample NPCs from this chapter or in-
In Elegy gameplay, your character’s abilities and             vent your own. Because NPCs don’t require stats
actions take center stage, and NPCs are portrayed             beyond Rank, they’re quick and easy to bring to
mainly through fiction rather than mechanics.                 life. Imagine who they are, what they want, and
They have only one mechanical stat, their Rank,               how they might act, and play from there. If you’re
which represents their durability in combat, skill            not sure, consult the Oracle for clues about their
and influence.                                                nature or intentions. Then, decide what feels true
                                                              to the fiction.
When NPCs appear in your story or assist you in
facing challenges, focus on how they shape the                NPCs may become long-term presences in your
story and your decisions. Treat them as real char-            story. When they stick around, take notes about
acters, not just tools: give them quirks, desires,            what you’ve learned about them, especially if you
and a voice. Use the Oracle to guide their choices,           create a Connection.
reactions, and performance.                                   This chapter provides sample NPCs organized by
They might help or hinder you. They might be                  category, offering many starting points. Aside from
charming, unreliable, loyal, or dangerous. Let them           their Rank, you’ll also encounter a brief description
evolve as the story unfolds, but always keep the              and two traits:
spotlight on your character and their closest allies.         • Drives: What this NPC usually seeks above all.
                                                              • Means: What tools, assets, powers, skills or
                                                                resources this NPC employs to fulfill their
                                                                objectives, and what kind of leverage you may
                                                                gain from them if you join forces.
                                                              When including them in your narrative, you don’t
                                                              need to limit yourself to the sample characteris-
                                                              tics. Customize as you wish, shaping your world
                                                              according to your preferences.
                                                              Elevate the Rank by one (up to a maximum of
                                                              5) for exceptionally powerful NPCs. Conversely,
                                                              lower the Rank by one (to a minimum of 1) for less
                                                              formidable adversaries.
                                                              When a Connection NPC goes into dangerous
                                                              missions with you, you create a Pulse track for
                                                              them, representing their overall durability.

Elder
Rank 4. A vampire dead for 151 to 300 years. Most of
her humanity is gone: she has forgotten nearly all of
her mortal life, and in many ways she is a different
person entirely. Mortals are hard for her to relate to,
and she sees them as little more than toys or food.
Drives
• Leave a lasting legacy in the underworld
Means
• Gifts she has discovered new powers for
• An official position
• Influence over all vampires in the city, even if
  unofficial
• A residence in the most expensive parts of the city
• Deep insight into both the vampire and the
  human condition
• Power, knowledge and resources accumulated
  over the years

E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )            110

Ghosts
Ghosts are the spirits of dead mortals bound to the material plane by trauma and unfinished busi-
ness. They usually speak with unbodied voices and manifest through drops in temperature, electrical
interference, bad luck, hallucinations, moving objects, or brief sensations of touch with no one there.
Ghosts can’t be harmed, and unless one has already made contact, she can only be reached directly
through magic. A ghost can only be removed from a place by completing a Mission for her, one that
resolves the matter binding her to the earth. The Mission should be one Rank higher than the ghost.

Recent Ghost                                                    Wraith
Rank 1. The ghost of someone dead for up to 50 years.           Rank 2. After 50 to 100 years, a ghost has lost much

dead. Within these limits, though, she is lucid. Recent         trauma, too confused to understand. Wraiths are
ghosts are hurt, bitter, and angry, but rarely violent.         miserable, cynical, and borderline sadistic.

Creating Factions
Every faction has:                                         Not all alignments are present in every city. For
• A name;                                                  example, if there is no Concord, there can be no

ALIGNMENT                                                  Often, this council is led by one of them who holds
A faction’s alignment indicates how it relates to          final authority over decisions: she is the Queen.
other factions in the big picture.                         VALUES

                                                           Values summarize the faction’s characteristics
                                                           and distinguish it from the others. They rep-
                                                           resent what the faction seeks in general and are
                                                           reflected in their practices. They may influence
                                                           what kind of mortal they choose to turn, who they
                                                           associate with, what resources or skills they use to
                                                           reach their means, and what influence they seek.




E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )             116
Values do not represent the totality of a faction’s ideology. They are simply its focus; what defines it
most. For example, just because a faction doesn’t have Beauty as a value, it doesn’t mean it disdains or
ignores aesthetics, but simply that it’s not one of its priorities.
Each faction has three values. Below are some samples and their meaning. You may also create your
own, or make them more specific to further personalize the faction. For example, if a faction is deeply
tied to music but not to other arts, instead of choosing the value Beauty, you may choose Music.
Faction values and name ideas

Oracles
Introduction
When you need details about your world or the               Oracles are divided into the following sections:
development of your narrative, you may leave it             • General: for yes/no or open-ended questions.
to luck and ask the oracle: roll dice and compare           • Characters: for details about those you meet,
them to random tables created to solve questions.             already know, or even yourself.
Some oracles have more specific purposes (names             • Places: for details about locations you enter
of characters, types of places, nightclub activity,           or discover.
etc.), which you may use for direct answers. When           • Faction: for details about vampire political
none apply to your situation, or when you want a              groups.
quicker, but more more abstract result) you may             • Story: for the direction of your plot.
turn to the General oracles.                                • Ambience: for background activity and
                                                              surrounding elements.

Turning
WHO WERE THEY TO YOU?

         Someone under your guidance. They looked up to you with admiration and aspired to be more like
   1
         you. They may have been an apprentice, a student, a younger sibling, etc.

         Someone you hated. You may or may not have had a pleasant relationship in the past, but in the
   2
         present, you never saw eye to eye. They may have been a rival, a boss, an ex, etc.

         A service provider. You used to be their client. They may have been a plumber, therapist, doctor,
   3
         waiter, etc.

         A colleague or contact. Your relationship was based on mutual academic or professional
   4
         background. They may have been a classmate, a coworker, a business contact, etc.

         An acquaintance. You knew each other by name and even said hi, but you never talked much. They
   5     may have been a significant other’s cousin, a friend of one of your parents, your neighbor you saw
         now and then, etc.

   6     A complete stranger. You met them for the first time right before they turned you.

         A mentor. They were a figure of authority and wisdom to you, like a more experienced colleague, a
   7
         professor, etc.

         A lover. You used to fill each other with lust and affection. They may have been a significant other,
   8
         a friend with benefits, a situationship, etc.

         A great friend. You could always count on each other in any situation. They may have been a
   9
         roommate, a friend, a best friend, etc.

         A relative. Close or distant, even in life you had blood ties. They may have been a cousin, aunt,
  10
         uncle, sibling, etc.

WHY DID THEY TURN YOU?

 01–12   They wanted a companion in undeath and asked you.

 13–25   You were dying, or sick, and it was the only way to keep you alive.

 26–37   They almost drained you, and in an act of desperation, turned you.

 38–50   They did it to make you suffer or to punish you for something.

 51–62   You never knew. They disappeared without a trace right after you were turned.

         They wanted to ensure some kind of legacy and saw you as an excellent
 63–75
         candidate, and you wanted to be more like them.

 76–87   They wanted access to your useful skills and assets, and you were convinced or coerced by them.

 88–00   They believed you’d have a better existence in undeath, and proposed turning you.

Actual play
E LE GY   4 e B e ta M a n u a l ( v 3 )       156
Introduction
This chapter demonstrates what playing Elegy
actually looks like. It walks through character,                 White frames present the narrative.
world, and city creation, then plays a short first
session from start to finish.

                                                              Grey boxes quote rules and show dice rolls.

                                                             Red stars indicate mechanical consequences
                                                              of a situation or action roll.
                                                            ~ Eyes indicate oracle answers



Preparing the game
World Creation
Before anything else, the player picks her Truths to shape the world. She decides:
• Vampirism is believed to be the result of a               • Hunting territory is limited to specific feeding
  failed ritual. Dracula was a sorcerer in the                grounds defined by the authorities. She likes the
  story, and she wants to explore vampirism                   idea of vampires having their own kind of pub.
  from this magical angle.                                  • Sunlight reduces a vampire to a pile of bones.

  doesn’t want to justify the powers she picks.               maybe one goes missing, maybe someone
• The Population of vampires among mortals is                 plants one, maybe one is discovered.
  medium. Enough to interact with, not enough               • District access requires announcing yourself
  to dominate mortal society.                                 at a border post to linger in another faction’s
• The Political Landscape is marked by division,              territory. Freedom of movement, but with
  every faction independent from each other.                  tension.
  This keeps the political layer simple while still         • Witches are uncommon. Cool to have, but
  allowing for several factions with different                she doesn’t want them shaping the city. Same
  agendas.                                                    reasoning for Vampire hunters (uncommon,

  obedience.                                                  outskirts. The territorial tension between

  Yasmin was in life.

  undeath. When she realized how vulnerable
  she was, she learned to fight.

Rank 3) and Blanca (a young vampire friend she’ll
develop in gameplay; Rank 2).
She scores her attributes as following: +3 on
Charm, +2 on Soul and Mind, and +1 on Body.
Finally, she rolls for a slumbering place, and decides
an apartment in a derelict building is her home.

                                                        Notice that the action she’s attempting is directly
THE ACTION ROLL                                         aided by one of her Aspect’s abilities.
When you need to resolve an uncertain or
risky situation related to your character’s               SOCIALIZATION (EXPERTISE)
efforts, you make an action roll. To make an              Starting ability: When you roll to fit in social
action roll, roll your action die (1d6) and two           situations or create rapport, add +1
challenge dice (2d10).

                                                          ASPECTS GIVE YOU RUSH

                                                          When you get any Success in a roll directly
          Action Die    Challenge Dice
                                                          aided by one or more Aspects you own, gain
Then, add...                                              an extra +1 Rush.

• ...the value of appropriate Attribute for
  this action. When rules tell you to roll              The roll Yasmin is doing is directly aided by an
  with an Attribute, that's the one you add.            Aspect. In this case, an expertise. This means that,
• ...the cumulative value of any other                  if she gets a Success, she gains +1 Rush.
  bonus you may have, from Aspects or                   What she’s attempting to do fits into the standard
  previous rolls.                                       action Gather Information.
Then, compare your action score to each
challenge die individually. Your action score
must be greater (not equal) than a challenge
die to beat it.
Results

The result of your roll depends on how many
challenge die your action score beats.
• If your action score beats both challenge
  dice, it’s a Stylish Success. You get what
  you want, the way you want.
• If it beats only one, it’s a Flat Success.
  You get what you want, but with side
  effects, reduced effectiveness, or a
  moderate cost.
• If it beats neither, it’s a Failure. Your
  assumptions were wrong, a brief triumph
  costs too much, or you simply fail.

FEEDING

To restore your Blood, feed on a prey you’ve
met, hunted, or subdued: roll with Soul to
stop at the right time.
Stylish Success: Gain 3 Blood. If Starving,
instead gain 2 and erase the condition. If your
source is an animal, gain 1 less. If you fed from
this victim yesterday, they become extremely
weak; you must wait two days before feeding
from them again.
Flat Success: Also lose 1 Rush.
Failure: If not Starving, treat it as a Flat Suc-
cess; otherwise, they don’t resist: restore all
your Blood, unmark Starving, then Try Your
Conscience at the end of this scene. If instead
you're feeding from preserved blood, lose 1
Rush and gain 1 less Blood.


   1     +2           3     VS     5         8
         Soul                         Failure
                             (treated as Flat Success)

  When you make an action roll and both chal-                           She didn’t plan for this obstacle, but getting past

  When you get a match, something special hap-

  If any of your Aspect abilities present a prompt
  for a match in this specific context, you may
  follow it. If more than one prompt applies,
  you may follow any (or multiple) as long as it
  makes narrative sense.
  Otherwise, in addition to your roll results,
  you are met with a surprise. If you scored a…
  • Stylish Success, the surprise is neutral:
    roll on the Twist table.
  • Failure, the surprise is negative: roll on
    the Misfortune table.
  • Failure, and your action die scores
    higher than your Blood, instead, your
    predatory nature takes over: roll on the
    Impulse table or pick the most fitting or
    interesting option.

TRYING YOUR STANDING
                                                         The dawn breaks. In her pitch black
At the end of a night where you violated                 apartment, Yasmin’s movements fade
the laws or customs of vampire society, roll             away with the stars. Eternity will be back
with Charm to maintain your standing among               tomorrow, and will bring newfound perils,
your authorities. Actions that trigger this roll         but right now, it mercifully lets Yasmin
may include:                                             drift into the peaceful sleep of the dead.
• Breaking the laws or customs of the
  ruling authority
• Showing disrespect or defiance toward an
  elder or superior
• Exposing vampire secrets to outsiders
• Leaving a witness or unfinished business
  that could expose the community
• Failing or refusing a duty assigned by
  vampire authority
• Acting against the interests of a faction
  that holds power over you
Stylish Success: They turn their eye or let it
slide. Next night, it’s like nothing happened.
Gain +1 Rush.
Flat Success: They let it pass, but uncom-
fortably. Next night, you might feel an air of
awkwardness between you and authorities,
but nothing serious. Gain no Rush.
Failure: They condemn and punish you.
You awaken to the written warning from an
authority you disrespected; suffer the first
unmarked condition in the following cascade.


     2 +3            8    VS    5         0
         Charm                      Failure

  step in line. Reduce your max Rush by 1
  until next time you slumber. When you
  do, you recover: erase the condition.
